I just keep worrying that I'm basically failing as a husband and a father, and I really need some help getting it under control.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any episodes of intense fear or discomfort that reached a peak within minutes?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really had anything like that. It doesn't usually hit me all at once or peak in a few minutes like you're saying. It's more just a constant, heavy stress that's always there, running in the background all day.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself avoiding certain activities or situations because of your worries?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I don't avoid things, exactly. I mean, I still go to work, I still try to do stuff around the house... it's just that while I'm doing those things, the worry is always there. I don't stay away from situations, I just stress out the whole time I'm in them.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any physical symptoms like headaches or fatigue along with your worries?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. I mean, I'm tired, but I wouldn't say I have unusual fatigue or anything. And I don't really get headaches. It's mostly just... just the constant worrying that's the problem.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or depressed lately?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I feel sad. I don't really get depressed... it's not like a heavy, sad feeling or anything like that. It's just that my mind won't shut off. So, no, I don't think I'm depressed, just... constantly stressed out about everything.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any recurring thoughts that you can't get out of your head, or feel compelled to repeat certain behaviors over and over?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, my mind is always racing with different worries, like I mentioned, but it's not like I'm stuck on one specific thought or image that I can't shake. And I definitely don't feel compelled to repeat behaviors or do things over and over. It's just the constant stressing about regular life stuff... I don't have any routines or rituals like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any thoughts that keep coming back to you even when you try to ignore them or think about something else?\n\nPatient: No, I think that's pretty much what I was just saying. It's not one specific thought that keeps forcing its way back in. It's just... a constant stream of worrying. If I try to think about something else, or, you know, focus on my work, my brain just finds a new thing to stress about. But it's not like a single thought I'm trying to block out.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any thoughts that are intrusive or unwelcome, that you try to suppress or ignore?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, it really is just what I was saying before. It's not like I have these weird or shocking thoughts that pop into my head that I have to fight off. It's just normal stuff... work, bills, my family... but the worry about all of it just never stops. So, no, nothing intrusive like that, just the constant stressing.\n\nDoctor: Is your worrying centered around just one or two main areas of your life, or is it spread out across many different things?\n\nPatient: It's definitely spread out across a lot of different things. Honestly, that's really why I came in to see you. I just can't stop worrying about everything at once. \n\nIt's my marriage, wondering whether I'm slipping at work, the bills... even just the house projects I keep starting and dropping. It all just blends together into this constant fear that I'm basically failing as a husband and a father. So, yeah, it's not just one or two things. It feels like it's everything.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're able to control your worrying most of the time?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Even when I tell myself to just focus on the code right in front of me, or... or try to just breathe through a workout, I can't stop it. One concern just hooks into another. Before I know it, I'm twenty steps ahead, imagining a bad performance review turning into me losing my job, and then my family being worse off because of me. I really have no control over it.\n\nDoctor: Has your constant worrying been interfering with your ability to function and enjoy your daily activities?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it definitely is. It's... it's affecting pretty much everything. At work, my manager has actually started documenting missed architectural details in my code, which is really bad. And at home, it's just as bad. I'm leaving all these house projects half-finished. I'm just so distracted all the time that I'm not really present with my wife, and it's causing arguments that just keep escalating. So, yeah, it's definitely getting in the way of my life.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I don't avoid things, exactly. I mean, I still go to work, I still try to do stuff around the house... it's just that while I'm doing those things, the worry is always there. I don't stay away from situations, I just stress out the whole time I'm in them.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any physical symptoms like headaches or fatigue along with your worries?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. I mean, I'm tired, but I wouldn't say I have unusual fatigue or anything. And I don't really get headaches. It's mostly just... just the constant worrying that's the problem.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or depressed lately?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I feel sad. I don't really get depressed... it's not like a heavy, sad feeling or anything like that. It's just that my mind won't shut off. So, no, I don't think I'm depressed, just... constantly stressed out about everything.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any recurring thoughts that you can't get out of your head, or feel compelled to repeat certain behaviors over and over?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, my mind is always racing with different worries, like I mentioned, but it's not like I'm stuck on one specific thought or image that I can't shake. And I definitely don't feel compelled to repeat behaviors or do things over and over. It's just the constant stressing about regular life stuff... I don't have any routines or rituals like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any thoughts that keep coming back to you even when you try to ignore them or think about something else?\n\nPatient: No, I think that's pretty much what I was just saying. It's not one specific thought that keeps forcing its way back in. It's just... a constant stream of worrying. If I try to think about something else, or, you know, focus on my work, my brain just finds a new thing to stress about. But it's not like a single thought I'm trying to block out.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any thoughts that are intrusive or unwelcome, that you try to suppress or ignore?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, it really is just what I was saying before. It's not like I have these weird or shocking thoughts that pop into my head that I have to fight off. It's just normal stuff... work, bills, my family... but the worry about all of it just never stops. So, no, nothing intrusive like that, just the constant stressing.\n\nDoctor: Is your worrying centered around just one or two main areas of your life, or is it spread out across many different things?\n\nPatient: It's definitely spread out across a lot of different things. Honestly, that's really why I came in to see you. I just can't stop worrying about everything at once. \n\nIt's my marriage, wondering whether I'm slipping at work, the bills... even just the house projects I keep starting and dropping. It all just blends together into this constant fear that I'm basically failing as a husband and a father. So, yeah, it's not just one or two things. It feels like it's everything.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're able to control your worrying most of the time?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Even when I tell myself to just focus on the code right in front of me, or... or try to just breathe through a workout, I can't stop it. One concern just hooks into another. Before I know it, I'm twenty steps ahead, imagining a bad performance review turning into me losing my job, and then my family being worse off because of me. I really have no control over it.\n\nDoctor: Has your constant worrying been interfering with your ability to function and enjoy your daily activities?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it definitely is. It's... it's affecting pretty much everything. At work, my manager has actually started documenting missed architectural details in my code, which is really bad. And at home, it's just as bad. I'm leaving all these house projects half-finished. I'm just so distracted all the time that I'm not really present with my wife, and it's causing arguments that just keep escalating. So, yeah, it's definitely getting in the way of my life.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or on edge?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. I feel wound tight almost all day, really. It's... it's like my shoulders are just completely locked up and I'm constantly bracing for something. I catch myself pacing a lot, especially between meetings at work. And even when I'm trying to do something I enjoy, like at my chess club, I'm just constantly tapping the pieces. It just feels impossible to sit still right now.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

    "content": "Return the JSON now."

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi. Well, I came in because I just can't seem to stop worrying about everything at once. It's... I don't know, it's just constant right now. \n\nI'm always stressing about my marriage, whether my performance is slipping at work, the bills... even these house projects I start and just keep dropping. I just keep worrying that I'm basically failing as a husband and a father, and I really need some help getting it under control.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any episodes of intense fear or discomfort that reached a peak within minutes?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really had anything like that. It doesn't usually hit me all at once or peak in a few minutes like you're saying. It's more just a constant, heavy stress that's always there, running in the background all day.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself avoiding certain activities or situations because of your worries?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I don't avoid things, exactly. I mean, I still go to work, I still try to do stuff around the house... it's just that while I'm doing those things, the worry is always there. I don't stay away from situations, I just stress out the whole time I'm in them.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any physical symptoms like headaches or fatigue along with your worries?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. I mean, I'm tired, but I wouldn't say I have unusual fatigue or anything. And I don't really get headaches. It's mostly just... just the constant worrying that's the problem.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or depressed lately?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I feel sad. I don't really get depressed... it's not like a heavy, sad feeling or anything like that. It's just that my mind won't shut off. So, no, I don't think I'm depressed, just... constantly stressed out about everything.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any recurring thoughts that you can't get out of your head, or feel compelled to repeat certain behaviors over and over?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, my mind is always racing with different worries, like I mentioned, but it's not like I'm stuck on one specific thought or image that I can't shake. And I definitely don't feel compelled to repeat behaviors or do things over and over. It's just the constant stressing about regular life stuff... I don't have any routines or rituals like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any thoughts that keep coming back to you even when you try to ignore them or think about something else?\n\nPatient: No, I think that's pretty much what I was just saying. It's not one specific thought that keeps forcing its way back in. It's just... a constant stream of worrying. If I try to think about something else, or, you know, focus on my work, my brain just finds a new thing to stress about. But it's not like a single thought I'm trying to block out.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any thoughts that are intrusive or unwelcome, that you try to suppress or ignore?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, it really is just what I was saying before. It's not like I have these weird or shocking thoughts that pop into my head that I have to fight off. It's just normal stuff... work, bills, my family... but the worry about all of it just never stops. So, no, nothing intrusive like that, just the constant stressing.\n\nDoctor: Is your worrying centered around just one or two main areas of your life, or is it spread out across many different things?\n\nPatient: It's definitely spread out across a lot of different things. It all just blends together into this constant fear that I'm basically failing as a husband and a father. So, yeah, it's not just one or two things. It feels like it's everything.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're able to control your worrying most of the time?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Even when I tell myself to just focus on the code right in front of me, or... or try to just breathe through a workout, I can't stop it. One concern just hooks into another. It's more just a constant, heavy stress that's always there, running in the background all day.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself avoiding certain activities or situations because of your worries?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I don't avoid things, exactly. I mean, I still go to work, I still try to do stuff around the house... it's just that while I'm doing those things, the worry is always there. I don't stay away from situations, I just stress out the whole time I'm in them.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any physical symptoms like headaches or fatigue along with your worries?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. I mean, I'm tired, but I wouldn't say I have unusual fatigue or anything. And I don't really get headaches. It's mostly just... just the constant worrying that's the problem.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or depressed lately?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I feel sad. I don't really get depressed... it's not like a heavy, sad feeling or anything like that. It's just that my mind won't shut off. So, no, I don't think I'm depressed, just... constantly stressed out about everything.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any recurring thoughts that you can't get out of your head, or feel compelled to repeat certain behaviors over and over?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, my mind is always racing with different worries, like I mentioned, but it's not like I'm stuck on one specific thought or image that I can't shake. And I definitely don't feel compelled to repeat behaviors or do things over and over. It's just the constant stressing about regular life stuff... I don't have any routines or rituals like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any thoughts that keep coming back to you even when you try to ignore them or think about something else?\n\nPatient: No, I think that's pretty much what I was just saying. It's not one specific thought that keeps forcing its way back in. It's just... a constant stream of worrying. If I try to think about something else, or, you know, focus on my work, my brain just finds a new thing to stress about. But it's not like a single thought I'm trying to block out.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any thoughts that are intrusive or unwelcome, that you try to suppress or ignore?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, it really is just what I was saying before. It's not like I have these weird or shocking thoughts that pop into my head that I have to fight off. It's just normal stuff... work, bills, my family... but the worry about all of it just never stops. So, no, nothing intrusive like that, just the constant stressing.\n\nDoctor: Is your worrying centered around just one or two main areas of your life, or is it spread out across many different things?\n\nPatient: It's definitely spread out across a lot of different things. Honestly, that's really why I came in to see you. I just can't stop worrying about everything at once. \n\nIt's my marriage, wondering whether I'm slipping at work, the bills... even just the house projects I keep starting and dropping. It all just blends together into this constant fear that I'm basically failing as a husband and a father. So, yeah, it's not just one or two things. It feels like it's everything.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're able to control your worrying most of the time?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Even when I tell myself to just focus on the code right in front of me, or... or try to just breathe through a workout, I can't stop it. One concern just hooks into another. Before I know it, I'm twenty steps ahead, imagining a bad performance review turning into me losing my job, and then my family being worse off because of me. I really have no control over it.\n\nDoctor: Has your constant worrying been interfering with your ability to function and enjoy your daily activities?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it definitely is. It's... it's affecting pretty much everything. At work, my manager has actually started documenting missed architectural details in my code, which is really bad. And at home, it's just as bad. I'm leaving all these house projects half-finished. I'm just so distracted all the time that I'm not really present with my wife, and it's causing arguments that just keep escalating. So, yeah, it's definitely getting in the way of my life.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or on edge?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. I feel wound tight almost all day, really. It's... it's like my shoulders are just completely locked up and I'm constantly bracing for something. I catch myself pacing a lot, especially between meetings at work. And even when I'm trying to do something I enjoy, like at my chess club, I'm just constantly tapping the pieces. It just feels impossible to sit still right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your worrying is realistic, or is it worse than what the situation actually calls for?\n\nPatient: I mean... I know logically that some of it is probably blown out of proportion. Like, realistically, one missed detail in a project isn't going to get me fired on the spot. But in the moment, it feels completely real. It feels like the worst-case scenario is definitely going to happen, and I just can't convince myself otherwise, even if part of my brain knows it's an overreaction.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your worrying is causing you a lot of distress?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it really is. It's... it's exhausting, honestly. I just feel so overwhelmed by it all the time. It's like I can't ever just relax or turn my brain off, and it's making me miserable. So, yeah, it's causing a lot of distress.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed your worrying affecting your relationships with friends or family members outside of your immediate household?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I guess I haven't really noticed it affecting them. I don't see my extended family or friends as much as I used to, mostly because I'm just so tired from work and everything else, but when I do talk to them, it seems fine. I haven't had any arguments or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing this constant worrying?\n\nPatient: It's been about nine months. I'd say it really started when the cloud migration project at work ramped up... and that was right around the same time the arguments at home started becoming a regular thing, too. So, yeah, it's been pretty constant since then.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. It's mostly just... just the constant worrying that's the problem.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or depressed lately?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I feel sad. I don't really get depressed... it's not like a heavy, sad feeling or anything like that. It's just that my mind won't shut off. So, no, I don't think I'm depressed, just... constantly stressed out about everything.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any recurring thoughts that you can't get out of your head, or feel compelled to repeat certain behaviors over and over?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, my mind is always racing with different worries, like I mentioned, but it's not like I'm stuck on one specific thought or image that I can't shake. And I definitely don't feel compelled to repeat behaviors or do things over and over. It's just the constant stressing about regular life stuff... I don't have any routines or rituals like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any thoughts that keep coming back to you even when you try to ignore them or think about something else?\n\nPatient: No, I think that's pretty much what I was just saying. It's not one specific thought that keeps forcing its way back in. It's just... a constant stream of worrying. If I try to think about something else, or, you know, focus on my work, my brain just finds a new thing to stress about. But it's not like a single thought I'm trying to block out.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any thoughts that are intrusive or unwelcome, that you try to suppress or ignore?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, it really is just what I was saying before. It's not like I have these weird or shocking thoughts that pop into my head that I have to fight off. It's just normal stuff... work, bills, my family... but the worry about all of it just never stops. So, no, nothing intrusive like that, just the constant stressing.\n\nDoctor: Is your worrying centered around just one or two main areas of your life, or is it spread out across many different things?\n\nPatient: It's definitely spread out across a lot of different things. Honestly, that's really why I came in to see you. I just can't stop worrying about everything at once. \n\nIt's my marriage, wondering whether I'm slipping at work, the bills... even just the house projects I keep starting and dropping. It all just blends together into this constant fear that I'm basically failing as a husband and a father. So, yeah, it's not just one or two things. It feels like it's everything.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're able to control your worrying most of the time?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Even when I tell myself to just focus on the code right in front of me, or... or try to just breathe through a workout, I can't stop it. One concern just hooks into another. Before I know it, I'm twenty steps ahead, imagining a bad performance review turning into me losing my job, and then my family being worse off because of me. I really have no control over it.\n\nDoctor: Has your constant worrying been interfering with your ability to function and enjoy your daily activities?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it definitely is. It's... it's affecting pretty much everything. At work, my manager has actually started documenting missed architectural details in my code, which is really bad. And at home, it's just as bad. I'm leaving all these house projects half-finished. I'm just so distracted all the time that I'm not really present with my wife, and it's causing arguments that just keep escalating. So, yeah, it's definitely getting in the way of my life.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or on edge?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. I feel wound tight almost all day, really. It's... it's like my shoulders are just completely locked up and I'm constantly bracing for something. I catch myself pacing a lot, especially between meetings at work. And even when I'm trying to do something I enjoy, like at my chess club, I'm just constantly tapping the pieces. It just feels impossible to sit still right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your worrying is realistic, or is it worse than what the situation actually calls for?\n\nPatient: I mean... I know logically that some of it is probably blown out of proportion. Like, realistically, one missed detail in a project isn't going to get me fired on the spot. But in the moment, it feels completely real. It feels like the worst-case scenario is definitely going to happen, and I just can't convince myself otherwise, even if part of my brain knows it's an overreaction.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your worrying is causing you a lot of distress?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it really is. It's... it's exhausting, honestly. I just feel so overwhelmed by it all the time. It's like I can't ever just relax or turn my brain off, and it's making me miserable. So, yeah, it's causing a lot of distress.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed your worrying affecting your relationships with friends or family members outside of your immediate household?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I guess I haven't really noticed it affecting them. I don't see my extended family or friends as much as I used to, mostly because I'm just so tired from work and everything else, but when I do talk to them, it seems fine. I haven't had any arguments or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing this constant worrying?\n\nPatient: It's been about nine months. I'd say it really started when the cloud migration project at work ramped up... and that was right around the same time the arguments at home started becoming a regular thing, too. So, yeah, it's been pretty constant since then.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a sudden feeling of intense fear or discomfort that came on quickly and reached a peak within minutes?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I feel anxious and stressed out all the time, but it's more of a constant, lingering thing. I haven't really had any sudden attacks of intense fear or anything like that. It's just a steady stream of worry.\n\nDoctor: Have you had to take any days off work or significantly cut back on your work or family responsibilities because of your worrying?\n\nPatient: I haven't taken actual days off, but... I'm just showing up so distracted all the time, and... well, it's a big reason why those arguments I mentioned keep escalating.
